--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page contains a transcription of the famous "Emerald Tablet" (Tabula Smaragdina), attributed to the legendary figure Hermes Trismegistus. This text is the foundation of Western alchemy, outlining the relationship between the macrocosm (the universe) and the microcosm (the individual). It is followed by a marginal commentary, likely by a later scholar or practitioner.</meta>

# Hermes, the Crown of Philosophers

The Philosopher says: the supreme power is in the middle. It is most true, that what is above is like what is below, and what is below is like what is above, for the performing of the miracles of the one thing <note>original: "rei unius". This refers to the "One Thing" or the Primordial Matter from which all things are created.</note>. Just as all things were created from one through the mediation of the one, so all things were born from this one thing by adaptation. Its father is the sun, its mother the moon. The wind <note>original: "ventus"</note> carried it in its belly; its nurse is the earth. Here is the father of all perfection <note>original: "thelesmi", a Latinization of the Greek *telesmos*, meaning completion or initiation rites</note> of the whole world. Its power is complete if it is turned into earth. You shall separate the earth from the fire, the subtle from the dense, gently and with great ingenuity. It ascends from the earth into heaven, and again descends into the earth, and receives the power of the things above and the things below. In this way, you will have the glory of the whole world. Therefore, all darkness shall flee from you. This is the strong fortitude of all strength, for it conquers every subtle thing and will penetrate every solid thing. Just as the world was created, from this shall come wonderful adaptations, the method of which is here. Therefore, I am called **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>Meaning "Hermes the Thrice-Greatest," a legendary Hellenistic figure combining the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth</note>, possessing three parts of the philosophy of the whole world. That which I have said concerning the operation of the sun and the moon is completed.

---

<margin>Aymaco <note>Likely a name, possibly a corruption of an Arabic authority like Hayman or Al-Majriti frequently cited in medieval alchemy</note></margin>
In the beginning of the humor <note>original: "humoris". In alchemy, this refers to the liquid or moist state of the matter being transformed</note> which you wish to become whole, show the <unclear>true</unclear> in the true, through which the other may become the same. First, he says the same regarding their soul, which is called the art of <unclear>mercury</unclear>. For it is proven that what is proven is divine; for that reason, it is most powerful through the divine, and it is called "most powerful" in the degree found. Because the sun is thus, it exceeds the natural wheel <note>A reference to the "Wheel of Nature" or the cyclical process of alchemical transformation</note> proper to you, and by means of this and other things, he prepares to touch the work.

<meta>Vatican Library stamp at bottom</meta>

<summary>This page presents the complete text of the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, the foundational text of alchemical philosophy, along with a marginal commentary regarding the "humor" and the "art of mercury." It explains the hermetic principle of "as above, so below" and the creation of the Philosopher's Stone through the union of solar and lunar forces.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Emerald Tablet, Alchemy, Macrocosm, Microcosm, Mercury, Sun and Moon, Thelesmi, Four Elements</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet, shifting from the core verses of the tablet to a detailed explanation of the "Stone" and the cosmological "One Thing." It explores the division of matter and the role of celestial influences (Sun and Moon) in the alchemical process.</meta>

It is said of the **Stone** <term>Stone; original: lapidis. Refers to the Philosopher's Stone, the goal of alchemy</term>. That which is in the above is through the earth that which is in the below. Through this, the **stone** is divided into two principal parts through the **Great Work** <term>Great Work; original: magisterium. The systematic process of alchemical transformation</term>. Namely, into the upper part which ascends above, and into the lower part which remains fixed. And all things in this are perfected, just as it is called "earth."

And so it is said that what is above is like what is below. For in this division, there is the power to penetrate the world's [secrets] of the **one thing** <note>original: "rei unius". This is the Primordial Matter or "first substance" from which the universe was created</note> through the stone and the Great Work, through those things named and finished. And through the superior and inferior, the whole stone is brought to life and generates vision. In this, there are separations, and all things in the world of the stone reach many eyes of the world. Thus indeed, all things existed from one through the mediation of the one.

He gives an example, saying: Thus indeed the entire world was in a spherical globe. In a spherical mass <unclear>meditation</unclear> <note>original: "meditacione". Likely a scribal error for "mediatione" (mediation), referring to the process of creation</note> whence it is called "one" through the operation of God. And just as it was one, all things come from this one. Thus all things were of nature's substance. They come forth from this spherical mass by a single adaptation, through its own unique spherical nature: earth, and water, and air, which was created by God in the mass.

And by its power alone, the stone is in the earth in nature. And thus that which is born <note>The text here reads "vinum" (wine), but in this alchemical context, it is likely a transcription error for "vivum" (living) or a metaphor for the "spirit of life"</note> generates at once through the perfect part, but it is crafted through the mediation of nature. The same produces the nature of the **sun** <note>original: "solis". In alchemy, the Sun represents gold and the active, masculine principle</note> with multiplication in the discovery of the perfect stone.

Therefore it follows: its father is the sun, its mother the **moon** <note>original: "luna". The Moon represents silver and the passive, feminine principle</note>. Through which, from natural nature, the spirits are interpreted as fit. And when it is said "it ascends," and then into this part it is crafted, it is necessary that the sun have its own spirit, which it then receives as fit. And through this, it ascends with that which is called [abundantly] to the **gold** <term>gold; original: aurum</term> itself. And that...

<summary>This passage provides a commentary on the Emerald Tablet, explaining how the "One Thing" was originally a spherical mass created by God, which then divided into the four elements and the "above and below." It identifies the Sun and Moon as the parents of the Philosopher's Stone and describes the movement of spirits during the Great Work.</summary>

<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Great Work, One Thing, Prima Materia, Sun and Moon, Alchemy, Four Elements, Hermes Trismegistus, Gold</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet. Having established the "One Thing" and its division into superior and inferior parts on the previous page, the author now explains the specific roles of the celestial bodies—the Sun and Moon—and the elemental stages of the Great Work, particularly the "white" and "red" phases.</meta>

It is the whole of the **silver** <term>silver; original: argentum. Associated with the Moon and the "white" stage of alchemy</term> that follows. Its mother is the **Moon** <note>original: "mater eius est luna". This is a direct quote from the Emerald Tablet, identifying the Moon as the receptive, feminine principle</note>. And it is not yet gathered in such a way in the **water of mercury** <term>water of mercury; original: aqua mercurii. Not common quicksilver, but a philosophical solvent used to dissolve metals</term>. This is what is called their mixture and nature in this art, leading toward the **quickening** <note>original: "vivificacione". The process of bringing "dead" matter to life through chemical change</note> to the **white** and to the **red** <term>the white and the red; original: albu[m] et rubeu[m]. These are the Albedo and Rubedo, the final two major stages of the alchemical process</term>. 

Then, since the **Sun** <note>original: "sol". The active, masculine principle and the father of the Stone</note> is shown to be more noble and more perfect among the perfect. And thus, since it is divine and precious throughout the world, being spiritual and composed by fire, it is the quickening force within the world. And this is what is shown: that the **red stone** then achieves its union. And the earth itself is called throughout the world the "earth of mercury." It is demonstrated. 

If indeed the silver is dissolved into the stone, then it remains fixed by itself and has no fruit. But it would be necessary for the whole to be received and fixed otherwise, and [the process] continues. And it is called throughout the world the "birthing woman" and the "dry." And so it is precious in that mixture which <unclear>is</unclear> that thing which exists in the harvest of the mixture and nature of those two. 

Wherefore, since gold is by itself and silver is by itself, they are difficult to fuse and liquefy. However, if they flow and liquefy together in a moderate and diverse way—and here he achieves the perfect solidities into gold—truly, let it be in the stone. In mercury there is then another way; through some **mastery** <term>mastery; original: magisteriu[m]. Also known as the "Great Work" or the secret knowledge of the alchemist</term> it will flow easily by mediation, though not covering the entire deity. And thus it is said that it will not cover the whole of what it is, which is not likely of the **tincture** <term>tincture; original: tincture. The potent liquid or powder capable of transmuting base metals into gold</term>. 

And now the final spirit, and here [is that] which may flow through mediation. The mercury of gold generates flight; their mercury is the nature which then received those two in the mercury of the stone. It came from the stone in the **belly of Venus** <note>original: "ventre veneris". While the Emerald Tablet usually says "the wind (ventus) carried it in its belly," this manuscript uses "Venus," the goddess/planet associated with copper and desire, perhaps suggesting a different symbolic path</note>, which is called "perfect." The belly carried it in its own belly: namely, the part which truly is **air**, and air into the true, and the true into **water**. And I speak of that which quickens the whole stone, and thus that which is true...

<summary>This page explains the alchemical roles of the Sun and Moon, the stages of the "white" and "red" work, and the necessity of mercury as a mediator to fuse gold and silver. It includes a variation of the Emerald Tablet's "wind" verse, here referencing the "belly of Venus."</summary>
<keywords>Moon, Sun, Mercury, White work, Red work, Tincture, Venus, Alchemy, Fusion of metals, Quickening</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus. The previous page discussed the celestial parents of the Stone (the Sun and Moon) and the Albedo (white) and Rubedo (red) stages. Here, the author focuses on the role of the "Earth" as the nurse of the Stone and the process of "fixing" the spirit into a perfect, physical form.</meta>

It carries and brings it back <note>original: "portet... et reportet"</note> in part through the **mastery** <term>mastery; original: magisteriu[m]. The completed alchemical process or the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone</term>. And then it follows that the nourishment of the stone and its **nurse** <note>original: "nutrix sua". This is a direct quote from the Emerald Tablet: "the earth is its nurse." It suggests the physical element that sustains the spiritual seed</note> is spoken of: namely, the **earth**.

Because when it is earth—that is, the medium in which it perfectly arrives as nourishment for the **mercury** <term>mercury; original: m[er]curio. Likely the "Philosophical Mercury," the living spirit or solvent used by alchemists rather than common quicksilver</term>—it then reaches a state that is called "perfect nourishment": **ammonium** <note>original: "am[m]on[i]u[m]". This likely refers to Sal Ammoniac (ammonium chloride), a key salt used to dissolve metals and "feed" the growing Stone</note>. And thus it appears on one side with mercury and another mercury, the first parts of the "son." It is concluded, if one speaks of the whole **tincture** <term>tincture; original: tincture[m]. A substance capable of transmuting or "dyeing" base metals into gold or silver</term>.

With mercury, the parts are first in the **whiteness** <note>the Albedo stage</note> and afterwards the **red** <note>the Rubedo, or final stage</note>. Then follows: "**The father of all perfection in the whole world is here**" <note>original: "pat[er] o[mn]is thelesmi toci[us] mu[n]di est h[ic]". The word "thelesmi" is a Latinization of a Greek term meaning "perfection" or "attainment"</note>, namely in this operation of the stone. This operation is what the **philosophers** <note>In alchemical texts, "the philosophers" refers to the ancient masters of the craft</note> call the "father of all perfection." It reaches a state where it becomes the perfection of the whole world—that is, the true property of every stone in this world.

Thus it is said: "**Its power is complete if it be turned into earth**" <note>original: "vis ei[us] i[n]tegra est si v[er]sa fuerit i[n] t[er]ra[m]". A core maxim of the Emerald Tablet, meaning the volatile spirit has been made solid and stable</note>. The stone is then perfect and **spherical** <note>the sphere was a symbol of cosmic perfection and the containment of all elements</note> if it is turned into "earth"—that is, into that part of the stone which previously existed through mercury, which is called the "true stone." 

The whole force of the stone is made perfect when it becomes the "earth" of the stone and is **fixed** <term>fixed; original: fixat[ur]. To make a volatile, vaporous substance solid and resistant to the heat of the fire</term> with it—that is, through the stone made perfect and pure by itself. And then, as it is a stone made perfect, it arrives in the operation at the **perfect ferment of mercury** <term>ferment; original: fermentu[m]. Just as yeast leavens bread, a "ferment" was thought to transform a large mass of base metal into gold</term> and a perfect compound. The whole part is found again in the **principle of moisture** <note>original: "p[ri]ncipiu[m] hu[m]oris". Alchemists believed all metals were born from a primal "moisture" or "water"</note>.

...until it reaches that part, namely the desired parts with the stone, so perfect that it arrives at the **multiplication** <term>multiplication; original: multiplicac[i]o[n]em. The stage where the Stone's power is increased so that a tiny amount can transform a vast quantity of lead</term> of the stone. Here it is multiplied; namely, through a part of the stone it is **quickened** <note>brought to life</note> and made perfect, as the text says: "**You shall separate the earth from the fire**" <note>original: "sep[ar]abis t[er]ra[m] ab igne". An instruction to separate the "gross" or impure physical matter from the "subtle" or pure spiritual essence</note>.

<summary>This page explains the stabilization of the alchemical spirit into "earth," interpreting the Emerald Tablet's instructions on "nourishment" and "perfection." It details how the Stone must be made "fixed" and "spherical" to reach the stage of multiplication.</summary>
<keywords>Emerald Tablet, Mercury, Earth as nurse, Albedo and Rubedo, Telesma, Fixation, Fermentation, Multiplication, Separation of Earth and Fire</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, moving from the concept of the Earth as the "nurse" of the Stone to the internal processes of purification, separation, and the union of Soul and Spirit. The author explains how the "fire of the philosophers" differs from common fire in its ability to refine the material without destroying it.</meta>

<margin>Note</margin> It is <term>purified; original: Purificatur. The process of removing impurities or "shadows" from the matter</term> by fire, by the <term>Spirit; original: Spiritu. The volatile or gaseous part of the alchemical substance</term> with great <term>fire; original: Igne. Usually referring to the controlled heat of the furnace</term> and <unclear>sweat</unclear> <note>original: "sudore". Likely referring to the condensation or "perspiration" that appears on the inside of the glass vessel during heating</note> and gradually, not through violence but with fire—just as with the <term>fire of the philosophers; original: igne philosophorum. A specialized, gentle heat, often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen</term>—you shall separate and dissolve, because dissolution is the first <unclear>separation</unclear> <margin>Note</margin> of <unclear>things</unclear>. 

It is <term>purified</term> from the fire by the <term>Spirit</term> and <term>Soul; original: Anima. The mediating force that connects the physical body to the volatile spirit</term> and the <unclear>multitude</unclear>. From the fire of air, water, and earth, the stone is seen—not just any stone, but a <unclear>world</unclear> stone, <unclear>most pure</unclear> like <unclear>blood</unclear> through the <unclear>act</unclear> <note>the successful completion of the chemical operation</note>. In this way, the stone is <unclear>fit</unclear> for <term>multiplication; original: multiplicationem. The stage where the power of the Stone is increased so it can transform greater quantities of metal</term>, and then it can be multiplied; it <unclear>melts</unclear> before the <unclear>sun</unclear> with a terrible power and in <unclear>hardness</unclear> it <unclear>softens</unclear> <margin>Note</margin> <unclear>hard</unclear> <unclear>bodies</unclear>. 

The <term>Soul</term> ascends into heaven and again descends to earth. It is very much to be noted that the <unclear>spirit</unclear> in the stone, in the first <unclear>operation</unclear>, <unclear>releases</unclear> <margin>Note, note, note</margin> <unclear>two</unclear> parts that are present, and another <unclear>fourth</unclear> part is to <unclear>remain</unclear> in our <unclear>fire</unclear>. Then at last there is the <term>Spirit</term>. There are <unclear>two</unclear> principal parts: namely, one which is the <term>Spirit</term> that ascends, which is of a <unclear>fixed</unclear> nature and <unclear>lacks</unclear> <note>perhaps "is free from"</note> the other, which is the <unclear>receding</unclear> spirit; the <unclear>fixed</unclear> part is called the <unclear>tincture</unclear> or <term>ferment; original: fermentum. A substance that acts like leaven, "ripening" the base metals into gold</term>, which changes and <unclear>ferments</unclear> the whole stone. 

<margin>Note, note</margin> Finally, in this, one must understand concerning the <unclear>spirits</unclear>—namely the <unclear>fishes</unclear> <note>original: "piscibus". A common alchemical metaphor for the volatile spirits that "swim" within the heated matter</note>—which are in the fire, so that it may be understood as such, but it must be done in our fire. Concerning that water which is not <unclear>fixed</unclear>, it is good; it <unclear>receives</unclear> nothing from the <unclear>fire</unclear> and <unclear>gives</unclear> to the stone that which was <unclear>previously</unclear> most pure, <unclear>emptying</unclear> out from all that <unclear>blood</unclear>, and through the <unclear>mastery</unclear> it <unclear>gives</unclear> to the whole stone until the <unclear>spirit</unclear> is not <unclear>fixed</unclear>. 

The <term>Spirit</term> descends <unclear>illuminated</unclear>, and this is what is said: "The <term>Soul</term> ascends," and it enters into heaven; after the stone itself has been thus <unclear>exalted</unclear>, it must <unclear>return</unclear>. The <unclear>dead</unclear> <term>Spirit</term>, with the oil <unclear>extracted</unclear> from the stone itself in the first <unclear>operation</unclear>—which is called the <unclear>water</unclear> of the stone—and by <unclear>applying</unclear> it so many times by <unclear>dissolving</unclear>, it <unclear>gives</unclear> to the <unclear>glass</unclear> <note>likely the vessel or the glass-like state of the material</note> by the virtue of <term>Penetration; original: Penetrationis. The ability of the alchemical agent to sink into and permeate the "pores" of a metal</term>. Then, with the <unclear>bodies</unclear>, to <unclear>exalt</unclear> the whole stone, it <unclear>again</unclear> descends to earth and it is <unclear>quickened</unclear>, <unclear>fixed</unclear> and <unclear>flowing</unclear> <note>original: "vivificatur fixus et fluens". This describes the "Red Elixir" which is solid yet melts easily like wax</note>.

<summary>This page describes the purification of the Stone through "the fire of the philosophers," emphasizing the separation and eventual reunion of the Spirit and Soul. It details the transition of the matter into a "fixed and flowing" state, capable of penetrating and transforming other bodies.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, following the description of the Stone's purification. It specifically addresses the passage concerning the Stone's power to receive both superior and inferior forces, and compares the creation of the Alchemical Stone to the biblical creation of the world from a "confused mass" or chaos.</meta>

And thus it is what is always said: that it defends the earth from the <term>fire; original: igni</term>, and so it receives the <term>superior and inferior power; original: vim superiorem & inferiorem. The idea that the Stone unites the celestial/spiritual forces with the terrestrial/physical forces</term>. It defends it, not so that the corporeal becomes spiritual substantially, but so that it defends the spiritual and fixed corporeal. 

And thus you have the glory of the whole world in this stone, thus opened <note>revealed through the alchemical process</note>; the perfection of this world is generated. <insert>ALL R</insert> All obscurity shall fly from you. All want and poverty [shall flee], because the stone thus opened annihilates all needs. This is the <term>strong fortitude of all fortitude; original: totius Fortitudinis Fortitudo fortis. A famous phrase from the Emerald Tablet describing the Stone's absolute power</term>. Because there is no comparison of this stone to other powers of this world. Because it overcomes every subtle thing and penetrates every solid thing. In "overcoming," it is understood that it overcomes the <note>original: "vincit vinum quod bibit". Possibly a metaphor for the Stone's ability to "absorb" and transform other substances, like a strong spirit overcomes wine</note> wine that it drinks and other things, when it is said: it overcomes and penetrates solids.

<margin>note note</margin> Through which just as the world was created. Through this example, the philosopher made a comparison for the stone, saying: "Thus the world was created," just as is the perfection of our stone. For first, the whole world and what is in the world was one <term>confused mass; original: massa confusa. Often identified with 'Materia Prima' or the primordial Chaos of Genesis</term>. At last, there is a sign through itself, through its own distinction by command, whence <unclear>nature</unclear> said in the mass of earth that the elements should be changed and proceed, through which the separations are divided—just as they are divided through the Holy Spirit from God—announced as water divided from the earth by separation, and the <unclear>heavens</unclear> were divided. 

Hence there shall be <term>wonderful adaptations; original: adaptationes mirabiles</term>. In <unclear>this</unclear> separation also was the world adapted; and because of this, among us who are in the operation of our stone through the joining of the thickened elements, the world here is opened to this manner, just as the world was adapted in <unclear>such</unclear> a way. Therefore I am called 

<margin>note note note</margin> <term>Hermes; original: Hermes. Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</term>, etc. Because the philosopher gave a comparison of this stone, he gives a second way concerning what our stone is in its first name: <term>Spirit; original: spiritus</term>, so that his sons arriving at this part may know from his name.

<summary>This page explains the "strong fortitude" of the Alchemical Stone, comparing its creation to the divine separation of elements from the primordial chaos (massa confusa). It emphasizes that the Stone provides "the glory of the whole world" and overcomes all physical limitations.</summary>
<keywords>Emerald Tablet, Hermes Trismegistus, Massa Confusa, Superior and Inferior Power, Four Elements, Separation, Fortitude, Creation of the World</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet, specifically moving from the general power of the Alchemical Stone to the specific nature of its composition. The text is now explaining the famous phrase "I have three parts of the wisdom of the whole world," relating it to the three kingdoms of nature (mineral, vegetable, and animal) combined in the one Stone. There is a Vatican library stamp present on the original manuscript page.</meta>

<margin>
man
</margin>

On the day of the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, because in no other way should it be made, according to the rule of 3 months; thus is made the **water from the animal** <note>original: "aqua ex animali," referring to substances derived from living organisms, often urine or hair in alchemical practice</note> which shall hold its rule just as was formerly seen from the <unclear>infant</unclear>. <meta>This page serves as the formal opening or preface of a treatise, likely concerning alchemy or natural philosophy, as indicated by the invocation of God and the citation of the 'Secretum Secretorum' (Secret of Secrets). Praise be to God, the most high Lord, glorious and sublime, who by His command has arranged and governed the heavens and the earth. This is the supreme and immortal God, to whom every movement of subordination in nature wisely obeys; it is He who gives wisdom to the wise. He is the fountain and origin <margin>perpetually, to whom it belongs.</margin> of everything, and the most sacred Christian Lord, illustrious through Himself as the beginning <margin>and through Him, He is the Lord, before whom all things are present.</margin> and the eternal Lord through Himself as the end. This is He who is the maker and creator of all things, whose will is executed everywhere, and to whom no other power can be said to exist. His kingdom is eternal and His works are all good; He rules and disposes all things through infinite ages. 

Wherefore, the <term>Metaphysics</term> <note>The author attributes this to Aristotle’s *Metaphysics*, though it mirrors the biblical Ecclesiastes 10:17: "Blessed is the land whose king is noble."</note> says well regarding all things to be done or made: Happy is the land whose lord is noble and one. In the name of our Lord Jesus Christ—who is the God of gods, the Lord of lords, and the King of kings of every creation and prince—I, [E.X.], <note>original: "ex."; likely the author's initials</note> though the least and most unworthy of all philosophers, seek His help, His liberality, His great mercy, and His goodness to reveal certain divine things. These are the secrets which have shone through the grace of God in revelations, and I intend especially to manifest them to all my friends in the world—to him who is well-learned in <term>natural philosophy</term> <note>The study of the physical world and its laws, considered a branch of philosophy until the modern era.</note>. 

Regarding this, <unclear>Rhazes</unclear> <note>original: "raso"; likely referring to the 9th-century Persian polymath and alchemist Abu Bakr al-Razi.</note> in the book of <unclear>prophecy</unclear>, his master, speaks here with authority, saying: "If [the philosopher] had left behind something other than what is called for here in his writings, they would require a complete rewriting." He lamented in such misery [of ignorance], just as I, at the beginning of this treatise, offer myself to you, my dear friends. 

This is done according to the custom of philosophers and out of respect for the prince of philosophers <note>A traditional title for Aristotle.</note> writing to Alexander regarding the precepts in the book *On the Governance of Princes* <note>original: "de regimine principum"; specifically the *Secretum Secretorum*, a famous medieval text purportedly containing Aristotle's advice to Alexander the Great on medicine, astrology, and alchemy.</note>. This was written by him who depicted it with his own sign, and in which he then wrote a fuller and more perfect work on secrets. May God, the most wise, therefore illuminate your reason and understanding.

...it has [its time], but not in haste; rather, it works through its own order, for as the <term>philosopher</term> <note>original: "philosopho"; likely referring to Aristotle or another classical authority on natural cycles</note> says: "Everything has its value in its own time," or that a work is perfected in more or less time.

For it is the ruler of the planets, and the body of the Moon... they are joined together... from plants, trees, and minerals, and through it every body is conserved and perfected. It is gold among the bodies. Gold is used by every body and held by it, and it is the <term>ferment</term> <note>original: "fermentum"; a substance that "infects" or transforms a larger mass, much like yeast in bread</note> of the two elixirs: the <term>red</term> <note>original: "rubi"; the elixir for making gold</note> and the <term>white</term> <note>original: "albi"; the elixir for making silver</note>. These cannot be transferred unless they are completed by the "dough" <note>original: "pasta"; the softened material state during the process</note>, which is not completed except through the ferment. And therefore, when the body is whitened... it is tested by that which is in it, namely its weight and <term>redness</term> <note>original: "rubiditas"</note>, and it does not aggregate... for these are the ferment from your spirit-body. For there is no ferment except the "dough," and similarly, human ferment is nothing but the body. 

This is called the "key" of the <term>philosophers</term> and of all the wise. According to <term>Aristotle</term> <note>original: "Ar̄y"; a standard abbreviation for Aristotle, the primary authority for medieval science</note> the philosopher, the Sun is the "eye of redness" in the permanent spirit, giving fixed heat and power in any mineral ore, in a living and light body... more than in any other...

### On the preparation of the body

Regarding the preparation of the **body**, I say the body can be well-prepared, both true and clean. And from whatever body you wish, in leaves or in filings, and this with **prepared silver** <note>original: "argento parato"; likely referring to purified silver or a specific silver alloy</note>. 1. four parts of **lead** <note>original: "plumbato"</note>, 3. of the body, and one of **salt** with the prepared [substance]; and place it in a leaden vessel on a leather [cover] over a small mold. Let it stand for a day and a night; make a heavy fire. Again, take half of it and grind it in its own mortar; put the other half in the hand, but it is said by the stone it flowers. 3. [parts of] horn, like a crow's horn, over it or over its own **hot water**, and then the salt dissolves and the powder of the body [settles]. Pour out the water drop by drop and you will find the body on the other side in the same vessel or another over a slow fire until it is ready for the work. Then first collect it from its own previous vessel, and then just like that, and it is said to be prepared now with **crude mercury** <term>mercurius: quicksilver</term> just as you will find lead in its own way.

### On soft bodies

Concerning **soft bodies**, do thus: it is of **Jupiter** <term>Iove: alchemical cipher for Tin</term> and **Saturn** <term>Saturno: alchemical cipher for Lead</term>. Pour into a tile just as they are melted; pour them in.

### On spirits and erring substances

Now we must speak of **spirits** <term>spiritus: volatile substances that evaporate or "fly" when heated</term> and **erring substances** <note>original: "peccantibus" (sinning/erring) – likely referring to impurities or volatile elements that "stray" or behave unpredictably during heating</note>. First, regarding **quicksilver** <term>argentum vivum: mercury</term>, namely how it and **arsenic** are to be turned into a single powder. 

Do it in this way: take a certain amount of **glacial alum** <term>alumen glaciale: transparent, ice-like crystals of potassium alum</term> and half as much **sal ammoniac** <term>sal armoniacum: ammonium chloride, used as a flux in alchemy</term>, and mix them together in a pan over the fire. Stir with a spatula until it appears as if it were water, then [mix] into it the prepared powder. 

Immediately place some of the salt—which has already been dried by the fire—into the bottom of the grinding vessel <note>original: "vasis pistatoris"</note> to the depth of one "reed" <note>a measurement of length or thickness, roughly a finger's width</note. Place the aforementioned powder on top of it, then place more salt over that powder as before, and finally place the **aludel** <term>aludel: a pear-shaped ceramic pot or series of pots used as condensers in the sublimation process</term> on top.

Seal the apparatus with the **lute of wisdom** <term>lutum sapientie: a specialized airtight sealant made of clay, dung, and other binding agents</term>; specifically, it must be sealed in every way against the moist smoke and vapor. Apply a lute made of earth—though not just any clay, but a mixture of good clay and clean horse dung—and mix them well together. 

After you have placed the vessel with all your material upon the **furnace** <term>fornellum: a small laboratory furnace</term>, give it a slow fire for half a day, until the "two smokes" depart through the holes in the upper part of the aludel. 

Regarding the vapor: they usually test it with a piece [of metal]. They say to use a knife placed over the middle [of the hole], leaving a small opening so the humidity can escape. When you see on the knife that nothing else from the material is rising through the hole of the vessel—which should appear white like milk—then the fire at the start was too strong. Moderate the humidity within it and gradually increase the fire for a total of twenty-four hours, so that you control the heat to prevent it from rising too quickly with the mass. 

Let it cool, then open the vessel and collect the substance from the upper vessel [the aludel] itself. Test it through several trials: place a small amount over live coals; if it produces smoke, then there is still more of...

Regarding **antimony** <term>stibium: a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid often used in alchemy to "purify" gold</term>, since a small amount then remains of the matter, you should have it thus. Renew it six times so that the sickness <note>original: "malz" (malum); likely referring to the "impurities" or "evils" within the raw mineral</note> with its name and knowledge might be given over; let it be cleansed for me from all filth and moisture above and beyond, so that it may never be substituted. Your weight depends on the manner of the procedure, and it should not be given unless it is illuminated. All labor and all fire that is in vain should be taken away, adding this knowledge in the first place. Through a small amount of purification of the metal, I know from this experience that unless knowledge differs from the light, it might be purified so that it purifies me. Rudolph says <note>original: "dūlph?" – possibly a reference to an alchemical authority like Rhazes or a contemporary author</note> not to illuminate, but to add with the weight of **sal ammoniac** <term>sal armoniacum: ammonium chloride</term>. Through this, the stibial part is taken away and the fire of the moon <note>original: "lūlī īgnē"; likely referring to silver (Luna) or a specific low-temperature fire</note> is brought to the **reverberation** <term>reverberatio: a process of heating a substance by reflecting heat from the furnace roof onto it</term>. I am not substituting it, but adding another place for the prepared metal.

Every truth truly consists in the middle. And two extremes cannot be joined without a medium, because the metal is the medium placed in this art between the parts of bodies that are both hard and soft. For they are not mixed with **alum** <term>alumen: a specific type of astringent mineral salt</term>, from which one cannot obtain **sal ammoniac**, **saltpeter** <term>sal petrae: potassium nitrate, a powerful oxidizing agent</term>, or **alkali salt** <term>sal alkali: a basic salt, often potassium carbonate or sodium carbonate</term>. As much as possible, let them be mixed in a mortar. After this mixture, which contains the potential of heat, place it in a glass vessel sealed with **lute** <term>lutum: a specialized clay or paste used to hermetically seal laboratory glassware</term> for a day and a night until it turns perfectly red. Then they evaporate, and finally they bubble in the act of distillation. By your movement, let it be distilled until it is entirely dissolved from the body. Through this water, it is coagulated with heat and moisture in a **Philosophical Egg** <term>ovum philosophicum: a round glass flask with a long neck, used for the long-term digestion of substances</term>. I say this heat is moved by the art of the philosophers with the force of heat and coagulation, which more truly makes the reason. It is not the destruction of the water, but the thing and the dissolved material go away entirely, remaining in the "dog" <note>original: "cane" – possibly an alchemical metaphor for the dregs or a specific part of the apparatus</note> because in heat and dryness it does not join. This is not a true coagulation, but a true dissolution which can be devoted to me for the sake of selling. All these things, when the metal is seen to be unmixed, are the best preparation and are valuable for health.

### Here begins the Book of Azoc, which is called the Philosophers' Measure <note>Original: "mensis philosophorum." While "mensis" usually means "month," in alchemical manuscripts it often refers to a "philosophical month" (a period of 40 days) or a "measure" (mensura) of their practice.</note>, because no better is found.

<margin>1. <unclear>Chapter</unclear></margin>
For just as you seek this doctrine, we will teach it to you more clearly than others. Indeed, no one is found who currently acts in such a way that this book is complete in all things, just as we have explained this doctrine. Furthermore, there are books not of this order, since another method is found through which no one performs the work correctly <note>The Latin "meacane" is likely a scribal error for "mechanice" (mechanically/correctly) or "mediante" (by means of).</note> in sequence. And now, since it has pleased you, we shall treat it in order. Therefore, let us speak first. It is already known to you that the first part of this art is distillation, which is the separation of earth, water, fire, and air from their natural state. This distillation is done by placing the stone in a cucurbit <term>cucurbit: a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation</term> and placing that in a pot containing ash. Under this, you should foster a gentle fire so that the water comes out; do not make the fire too strong. After this, take care to put the distilled water back in again. And this is done so that the distilled water remains with the [original] water, so that something <unclear>greater</unclear> remains in it and the water is not diminished. Indeed, after the water has been distilled, a stronger fire should be applied from below until the fire and air are distilled together. Let the distillation be performed. And when they are distilled, then take and save them, just as you did with the first, to be put back. Likewise, it is <unclear>praiseworthy</unclear> to keep it in the cucurbit until it recedes by itself. And this is the first order in this book.

<margin>ij. <unclear>Chapter</unclear></margin>
Secondly, you ought to know that many men err in this work because it seems easy. In the end of the work, however, they realize they have achieved nothing. For without the water which you will distill in the cucurbit and the alembic <term>alembic: the cap or "helmet" of a still that directs vapors into a receiving vessel</term>, this work cannot be performed. After this, once the water has been distilled, take the substance mixed with alceferan <note>Original: "alceferan," likely a corruption of "za'faran" (saffron), referring to "Saffron of Mars" (iron oxide) or a specific alchemical preparation</note> and distill it again. And God [gives] its <unclear>essence</unclear> which is within it. It is necessary that the cucurbit be positioned so that there is a cucurbit beneath a cucurbit, such that the likeness of the middle cucurbit is a cucurbit. The lower part of this should be narrow. The lower part of the upper one, however, has a mouth where both are joined, which you ought to fill by pressing in <term>zalchiferan</term> <note>Likely the same substance as "alceferan," here used as a "lute" or sealant to make the joint airtight.</note>. Likewise, join the water perfectly. And kindle a gentle fire beneath it. Clear water will indeed distill, and what you separate on both sides is the "second word" regarding that which we called water. You shall know that through these things all forms come into existence. Therefore, save this water in <unclear>vessels</unclear>.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions following the "Book of Thirty Books," focusing on the behavior of alchemical tinctures and the specific roles of sulfur, arsenic, and lead in the process of transmutation. ...so that silver may be transmuted into the color of gold, as copper <note>original: "es," referring to copper or its alloys like bronze</note> is transmuted into the color of silver. This is not the reddening <term>tincture</term> <note>an "elixir" or substance capable of changing the color and nature of a metal</note> we possess, nor is it the white whitening one. Furthermore, it should be known that the mixing of tinctures with hard, stony bodies is not possible until they are liquefied, and that the bodies may melt with the tincture; indeed, it is not possible to combine them before they are liquefied. 

However, when they are liquefied, the red or white tincture is not simply granted to us to produce that coloration. Thus, when the tincture meets the fire, if it does not produce red—or if it does not arrive, yet does not flee, but rather escapes and flees—it does not perform the work. Or if it does not arrive, it violates less but does not cleanse, nor does it <unclear>perfect</unclear> <note>original: "perimificat," likely a scribal variant of "perfects" or "completes"</note>, nor does it burn. Or if it cleanses and perfects with this, but does not remain, but for that reason, having been purified, it again escapes and is separated from them in an easy manner. 

And so, even if this aforementioned substance does not escape, but does not produce silver, it does not admit from them what it does not want; gold or silver is joined by means of <term>sulphur</term> and the remaining things by which gold is purified from silver. Thus it is necessary that it does not make the copper such that it cannot be joined by them—so that silver is not joined—and that the copper is joined through many methods. 

Therefore, we need a tincture to bring forth the tincture. Moreover, the white tincture, through mixture with stony bodies so that it is not joined with sharp, burning things, possesses the method of cleansing and does not permit the burning thing to separate the body for that reason. It is necessary for us to have these operations in these simple intermediaries through various means; and this is true regarding the parts and intermediaries. The medicine that has reached us has this impression, whence we again have the holding of <unclear>plonoe</unclear> <note>an obscure term, possibly a corruption of a Greek metallurgical term</note>, the account of which is as follows: 

And because red sulfur, taken by itself in a <term>dargana</term> <note>likely a specific type of furnace or alchemical vessel</note>, and white <term>arsenic</term> have this operation according to the recipes of my estimation, it is said that if there is truth in these things, another truth arrives regarding the fact that they were stones. By others mediating, whose account we have, it is certainly known to us so that we may find these operations through our own efforts in due time. They are inspired in the tincture to see how it may receive <term>lead</term>, which is profitable to liquefied things; lead consolidates the one, and lead coagulates the tincture, joined and fixed upon the fire, and joins silver for perfecting.

And let it join in that which is of the same nature of metals, mixed with that which was mixed with it during the time of the divine substance. And let it give all things with that which is in the perfected and solidified substance. And let it fix with that which remains with the fixed substance. ### Chapter 3: Concerning the Dyeing of that Tincture <note>original: "De tuccone [tinctione] illius tinctum"</note>

<margin>Chapter 3: Concerning its dyeing and fixation</margin>
Regarding the white work <note>the *albedo* stage, where the material is purified to a silver-like state</note>, it is perfected when we treat <term>quicksilver</term> <note>mercury, often symbolized by the god Mercury, considered the "spirit" of metals</note> to be perfectly white. And let us treat it so that, through its whitening, it adheres to the metallic bodies and penetrates into them. This is done so that through a perfect decoction <note>a process of slow heating or boiling</note> in vinegar with its medicinal additives, the whiteness penetrates into their interior parts without delay, just as silver is measured by its whiteness. And thus, when it has brought the quicksilver to an increased proportion, it will provide us with the impression and the operation of the work. 

The operation of this dyeing cleanses it from the fire and separates it from the process of generation <note>the formation of base or impure qualities</note>; not everything is like the matter of gold, which exists only through whitening. While it is permitted to seek this goodness, we excuse ourselves from the labor of the defects of burning by tempering the heat. This is done so that while the spirit <note>the volatile part of the substance</note> is within the spaces, it may be mixed with the permanent white liquor according to its disposition. Therefore, the purity of this thing and the separation of the waters from it are necessary, so that it may be calcined <note>reduced to a powder through dry heat</note> and pulverized, or shaped and ground. 

One must grind the spirit with these things and mix it; it should not be visible to the eye. And thus it may receive that which seems impossible with waters, but perhaps something harmful might happen to it which we cannot do without. This is but a step in the process, so that its decoction over a gentle fire may entirely separate the soul <note>the essence or volatile principle</note> from it, so that it remains in such a way that nothing further is possible, as a vapor from that which is perfect. I say this because if it is first tempered, its mass vanishes so that it may appear properly with a clean letter <note>possibly referring to a clear sign or result</note>. We do this so that we may mix with it the half which we cannot separate from it. But it is by reason that we take the moisture from the part and distill it from it.

<summary>This chapter details the "white work" of alchemy, focusing on the fixation and whitening of mercury through decoction in vinegar and controlled heating to ensure it penetrates base metals.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical "white work" (albedo). Having established the goal of whitening quicksilver on the previous page, the author now details the specific laboratory apparatus, the 10-day gestation period, and the chemical "mortification" of the metal using common salt and lime to achieve a pure, sublimated state known as "alucl."</meta>

Hence it is said: let it be hidden for 10 days. And so, when something of it burns together in the bottom of the instrument, and the purified thing is processed <note>original: "gematur," perhaps a scribal error for "geratur" (carried out) or "graduatur" (brought to a degree)</note> <unclear>until</unclear> the fifth stage, then that which is completely joined and sublimated as before is called "more white," in which there is no burning of sin <note>in alchemical terms, "sin" refers to the presence of base impurities or "sulfurous" dross that must be purged</note>. For in this work, we require a filiation <note>original: "filiacione," referring to the derivation or "begetting" of the new substance from the old</note>.

Therefore, let us take a smooth vessel in which there is a portion of it. Let us place in its mouth a stone in the flesh <note>possibly a mineral plug or a specific catalyst</note>, and let us place the vessel upon its side, with a cover over its head that is perforated in the middle, so that the vapor may better exit into that head. The vessel itself should be covered with a covering cloth <margin>as it is said in my book of <unclear>glosses</unclear> regarding that which is covered</margin> so that it is enclosed by its own mercy <note>a metaphorical way of saying "sealed safely"</note> to catch the substance when one judges that a single smoke is exiting from it. And let it be seen when it is first hidden by its own sublimity <note>its gaseous state</note>. 

In this state, we wish it to be sublimated. It is placed there through its own **mortification** <note>the chemical "killing" or breaking down of a metal's form to release its spirit</note>, so that it is seen with the burning things in the five primary parts consisting of <term>common salt</term> and <term>lime</term> and other things; because through these substances, the whole becomes well-dissolved in a mixture with them. Then, let us place it in the fire of the <term>oven</term> <note>original: "clybanis," from the Greek "klibanos," a small portable oven</note>. And this will also be done here: let us place it in a vessel cut off from sin <note>purgated of impurities</note>. 

Then let us take this vessel and seal it with the <term>lute of wisdom</term> <note>a specialized alchemical clay or sealant used to make vessels airtight</note> and place it into a pan of blacking <note>original: "atramen," likely a bed of soot or charcoal</note>. We know that through this, it is said that the **mortification of quicksilver** <note>mercury</note> occurs when it has been enclosed in the lute of wisdom. Next, take what is brought with you and leave it in the sublimation vessel; then you will have <term>alucl</term> <note>a term for a purified, sublimated salt or mineral spirit</note>.

And we sublimate it therein then, and as often as it is sublimated, we shall renew its mixture and return it upon the same stone. We shall do this at the entrance until it is moved and whitens. And who will sustain us for 10 days? And who will effect the process by itself up to 12 times, which is the <unclear>wish</unclear>? 

And then we shall place it beyond this in a milky woman <note>original: "femina lactea," a metaphor for a white furnace or a specific type of vessel/bath</note> smeared cautiously with the lute of wisdom. And we show over it that which is called the "roasted dew," through the whole sublimated substance by means of the ascending gifts <note>the rising vapors</note>. <meta>This page continues the technical discussion of arsenic and its "unctuous" (oily) properties. While the previous page established that arsenic can be prepared into a stable oil that penetrates metals, this folio examines how it behaves under fire—specifically, how to distinguish between the dross that burns away into ash and the volatile essence that survives through sublimation without being destroyed.</meta>

And if through fire there should remain dross and ash, it is that which results from **combustion** <note>original: "adustio"; the process of burning or scorching a substance until it is charred</note>. But this occurs when the moisture evaporates, leaving behind dry parts in the presence of flame. However, regarding these types of dryness that we have indicated and known, they are the point to which all **volatile substances** <note>original: "alata"; literally "winged things," a common alchemical term for substances like mercury, arsenic, or sulfur that vaporize easily</note> were drawn.

This is confirmed here by experiment: such a substance does not burn away. Indeed, if it is mixed with a colored substance, it resists combustion. But if it remains hidden by itself, it is not within its nature to evaporate in such a way that it leaves only ash behind. It does not burn away to nothing when contained within **liquefied bodies** <note>original: "corporibus liquatis"; metals or minerals in a molten or fluid state</note>.

Furthermore, whatever is held in the "mouth" <note>likely referring to the opening of a furnace or the neck of a flask</note> or in anything containing a "foreign heat"—which the substance itself resists—must be decocted for a long time with dry matter. This is done so that it does not burst into flame or burn. Know that since it does not dissolve, but instead forms a mixture with the dry matter, it does not lose its **oiliness** <note>original: "unctuositate"; the alchemical quality of being "fatty" or "oily," which allows a substance to "flow" into and permanently tint a metal</note>.

We observe that if a substance is naturally combustible and destructive, such dryness does not remain in it; rather, it adheres to it as we previously stated regarding the highest part. There is a certain species of these things where the moisture within such bodies is not driven out by the intense generation of dryness. Thus, the fire that turns them into vapor was "dry of dross," yet they did not separate; rather, there was a liquid quality within. Or, let the fire be of a gentle degree in the <unclear>vuo</unclear>.

And if the moisture was volatile and evaporates, it becomes a vapor—a smoke mixed with the dryness inherent in it. Because of this, the substance does not perish before it joins with that moisture. Furthermore, through this experiment—since it was a physical experiment—the highest dryness is refined through **sublimation** <note>original: "sublimatio"; the process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then collects again as a solid at the top of the vessel, effectively purifying it</note>. For it is certain to us that the essence is neither corrupted by the vapor nor does the vapor diminish its weight.

This moisture is thoroughly mixed with its dryness. We, who separate these parts through our work, must distance the "burning" from it. We observe many ways to achieve this. One method is **roasting** <note>original: "affatio"; slow heating or parching</note> with adhering substances that burn, which is essentially the same as sublimation. For undoubtedly, during sublimation, only that which is combustible burns away. It is clearly evident that the true essence does not burn. But if it lacks this stability and begins to burn, we must slow the process <note>original: "retardabimus"; we will delay/slow down</note> to return it to its original state.

<meta>This page continues the technical examination of volatile substances, specifically focusing on how to purify a "white" substance—likely arsenic or a mercury-based compound—so that it becomes resistant to the destructive power of fire. It builds on the previous page's discussion of "unctuous" or oily properties that allow these materials to penetrate metals like silver without scorching them.</meta>

The white substance is **sublimated** <note>original: "sublimat albu"; the process of heating a substance to turn it into vapor and then cooling it back into a solid to purify it</note> in such a way that no mixture of burning is present. And when this aforementioned substance is placed over silver, it does not scorch it, nor will it corrupt it by itself. And it was also found among us, with the aforementioned things, that when it was exposed, it remained gloriously <note>meaning it remained pure or stable in the fire</note>. Because of this, this work did not lack a superior power, for it melts and adheres. And that which, through its "vinosity" <note>likely a reference to a spirituous or volatile quality</note>, quickly burns—that is, through the process of **combustion** <note>original: "adustionem"; scorching or charring</note>—is either recently burnt or corrupted by the superfluous moisture of the substance. 

And we found that in this superior case, it will not burn. It should be known that through the continuity of moisture, it is said to be "perperic" <note>likely a scribal error or technical term for "imperishable" or "lasting"</note>, because the superfluous moisture and the combustible moisture are removed from it. This substance remains as a moisture that is far removed from dryness. Therefore, that moisture itself was dried out until it became a **liquefied oil** over the fire. Through this superior method, we have tested and found that it adheres. 

We also found that the danger of the white substance is that it appears not to be white. And then, in its liquefied state, it is so mixed that it cannot be separated from it, or it is tenaciously mixed with it. When we covered the head of the **vial** <note>original: "ampulle"; a glass flask used in alchemy</note>, we did not find any sharpness that would keep it from adhering to it. 

And we knew through this experiment, with the dominating power and the superior operation, that the white substance is completely whitened. Through what we see, we found it to adhere, and it appeared to us as we desired. And because it does not sublimate away, but we have directed it into an oil with other masters, the fiery power was removed from it. The mass of superfluous moisture and dryness evaporates, and there remains a moisture that is fixed in dryness, which was not there before; rather, it moves through itself and melts the substance. One should not wonder at this, as it is agitated <unclear>cinil</unclear> by oil or water when placed over the fire.

Furthermore, through this superior path, we did not find common **sulfur** in the red liquor for the white work. Indeed, you have something better: we found that **arsenic** with the white substance is a better operation, provided it is done by **unction** <note>original: "guctione/unctione"; the process of making a substance oily or spreading it like a salve</note>.

The essential <note>original: "quidditativa"; relating to the 'what-ness' or essence of a thing</note> substance is a type of **burning water** <note>original: "aqua ardens"; usually referring to distilled alcohol or a strong solvent used in alchemical extraction</note> as it is named by many; and regarding this matter, we do not inquire among ourselves about these things.

The fixed and bright definition of **gold** <note>original: "solis"; literally 'the sun,' the standard alchemical symbol for gold</note> is that which is not sought after by this art; it is the very thing that loosens and illuminates all physical bodies <note>original: "corpora phisicorum"</note>—that is, among us, through the work. Within this, the times achieved are [improved] and better, and their kinds remain within them for a long time by whatever means. For it is not possible for something else to be generated except from its own higher species, with which it can become one. It is necessary, therefore, that these be joined with the calcined materials. And thus what is called **Filings** <term>Limatura</term> <note>fine metal shavings or ground powder</note> is applied over the bodies. And we see, according to the light, how we may produce **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum vivum"; mercury</note> from silver, which is better; or from lead, or gold, or from a superior gold, or from copper. It is possible for us to take such a thing from silver, and it is white **ceruse** <term>cerusa</term> <note>historically white lead, but here referring to a purified white metallic powder</note>, which is the **powder** <term>calx</term> resulting from the whitening process. From gold, it is a red powder according to its use, which is the **powder** <term>calx</term> resulting from the reddening process.

And this is because it is not possible for the first motion to occur due to the generation of bodies. We see this in those things we deal with in our methods of operation. For that water itself generates the gold and silver of **Alchemy** <term>Alchemie</term>; and finally they are brought forth through **thickening** <term>coagulatio</term> and **freezing** <term>congelatio</term> <note>the process of making a liquid substance solid or stable</note>. <meta>This page continues the technical instructions for creating the Elixir, transitioning from the preparation of metallic powders (calxes) to the "penultimate" stage of the work. It describes the extraction of spiritual essences from roots and moves into Chapter 8, which explores "mineral waters" derived from organic sources like hair and blood.</meta>

<margin>
<unclear>...to the elixir...</unclear>
</margin>

For the present, we arrive at the penultimate stage of our science which is possible: namely, the spiritual matter <note>original: "res spiritualis"</note> which is the **Root** <term>radix</term> <note>the fundamental base or source material of an alchemical recipe</note> to be extracted. Let these be dissolved <note>original: "resoluantur"</note> so that from the creations there are made others who are not prohibited from these aforementioned elixirs with dissolution. Dissolution from their radical operations remains, rather, a nature of virtue <note>original: "virtute"; the inherent power or potency of the substance</note>. Since they meditate on what they wish by testifying to the experiments to be produced, they are not all, therefore, according to the same dissolution. 

Next, it is seen how this kind of species and root may become a spiritual matter, preparing and soon becoming [the elixir], being weighty and powerful. And let us know, therefore, that these **decoctions** <note>boilings or heat treatments</note>, insofar as they are boilings with heat for the sake of dissolution, must be administered through constant attention, which is also the same in the place of dissolution. According to this, then, all things are for testing what the substance had and what it did not have, and what might be endangered by the deliberate action of the unskilled, or by a defect in the work, or by actions that are not understood. It was therefore our intention to apply this heat in the highest powerful art of the **elixir**. 

It is brought forth, therefore, with the praise of the immense God, for they were the **Root of white** <note>a reference to the Albedo or 'whitening' stage of the work</note> **Quicksilver** <term>argentum vivum</term> <note>mercury</note> prepared with **Sulfur** <note>original: "sulphur"</note> and whitened **Arsenic** <note>original: "arsenicum"</note>. And in the composition, how they were mixed with the greatest Elixir; and they were the root of burning things, sulfur prepared with prepared quicksilver, and whitened sulfur and the **Powder of Gold** <term>calx auri</term>. To me, that is so that all things may be **Reddened** <note>original: "rubificata"; the final 'Rubedo' stage representing the perfection of the stone</note>. Then let them be placed and let the work of the elixir be noteworthy.

Chapter on other physical things

Afterwards we heard the intention of **Chapter 8. On mineral waters** which properly concerns hair, eggs, and blood, and many first things from which this elixir may be made. Let us begin to work, therefore, by taking common **Sulfur** and its impression in the higher bodies <note>celestial or more refined substances</note>. We find in them the spirit and the smoke of the bodies. For that sulfur, with the bodies and their covered proportions, strictly [affects] **Silver**. Namely, the "friend" of silver sublimated with common **Mercury** <note>original: "mercurio communi"</note>. We find, therefore, that common mercury is cemented by common mercury and by impression; let us know, therefore, that hair and blood and all [parts] of the devoted [materials] have their waters coagulated in salt. And let us know that when they wish to prepare the **tincture** <note>original: "tinctura"; the color-changing agent that transforms base metals</note> and the impression from them, it is not possible [without this knowledge].

...so that they are ground strongly with a strong **lye** <note>original: "lixivia"; an alkaline solution leached from wood ashes, used as a powerful cleaning and reacting agent</note> and boiled in water with vinegar until they are dried out. Then they should be sprinkled with **sublimate** <note>original: "sublimato"; likely mercuric chloride, a common reagent in alchemy</note> and ground with salt, **sal ammoniac** <term>sale armoniaco</term> <note>ammonium chloride, used as a flux and for its ability to sublimate</note>, **quicklime** <note>original: "calce viva"; calcium oxide</note>, and common sea salt. Then, having taken the **tincture** <note>the concentrated essence or color-giving principle</note>, let them be boiled in water in which strong lime was previously boiled, then for a long time until the water appears white as snow. Then in this way let it be whitened.

### Chapter IX: On Calcination

<margin>Chapter IX: On calcination</margin>
**T**hen let **quicksilver** <term>argentum vivum</term> <note>mercury</note> be taken with sal ammoniac and vinegar. And when it is united with the joining agents, let it remain for a long time until it receives the color of salt, with sal ammoniac and **sulfur**. And when this is well ground, continue until the quicksilver is sublimated from them entirely until a powder remains. Then, if at any time it is soaked for whitening in distilled salt water and ground and left with the fire until it becomes red—but let it not be too near the fire—because if it is soaked for reddening in ammoniacal water well, and then in the fire with the reds of sulfur or others from which it receives its color, being constantly roasted until it is made red, let it become a powder, and let it not be too near the fire.

### Chapter X: On Resolution

<margin>Chapter X: On resolution</margin>
**T**he method of procedure that we hand down for this **resolution** <note>original: "resolutione"; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid or breaking it into its constituent parts</note> is because it cannot be resolved into receiving things unless it is pure, like salt and sal ammoniac. And we have seen according to the order of operations concerning the **body** <note>original: "corpore"; the solid material or metal being worked upon</note> mixed with sal ammoniac and then resolved; and constantly seeking the soaking of the thing by pounding, and constantly with heating for a long time until the whole exists in the sal ammoniac. And then, if it does not flow over the fire by means of the resolved ammonia, you should seek another until it leaves the fire of the heart until it is finished. Then let them place it in a clay vessel which is like a head <note>likely the "alembic" or top part of the still</note>, and let fire be applied for as long as it takes until that smoke ceases and remains within it. Then let it be soaked in water. Afterward, let us do the same thing to it another ten times. Then, when we have seen it thus, let us act until it is found just as it should be, so that it melts over the fire when ignited. Then let it be dissolved in water and let nothing remain.

### Here begins the Book of the Perfect Mastery, Chapter 1

<margin>Here begins the Book of the Perfect Mastery</margin>
Since through the investigation of skillful study, it belongs to the sagacity of the most dear son [and] commentator to examine the masteries of things [and] to lead them to the highest knowledge <term>perscientia</term> <note>a term for complete or fore-knowledge, often used in alchemy to denote technical mastery</note>, to investigate the approaches of the art which are sought in these things; nor can it be done by [mere] arguments. For it is governed according to the led-forth [hidden] properties of nature. For one should not suspect that a thing operates unless one has known the apparent and manifest natures. Nothing is perfected in the art by chance; it is better that one should know the hidden natures of things without error, just as in this book it treats of hidden knowledge <note>original: "perscia occulta"</note> / and it is about that part of knowledge which is called **meteorology** <note>original: "metheora"; in the Middle Ages, this referred to Aristotle’s "Meteorologica," which covered atmospheric phenomena as well as the formation of minerals and metals within the earth</note>. For it speaks here of the knowledge of the air and the sea, of the elevation and repression of <unclear>terrestrial</unclear> and elementary <unclear>vapors</unclear>.

<margin>Concerning lower astronomy</margin>
You should know through this art that knowledge is of **lower astronomy** <term>astronomia inferior</term> <note>a common medieval synonym for alchemy, suggesting that metals on earth correspond to the planets in the heavens</note>. Just as there is astronomy [proper]. And that which is noted above, because it is the <unclear>mother</unclear>. For higher astronomy speaks of the perfect bodies in the firmament (such as the fixed stars which are called planets because they are carried in the firmament by their own motion, such as the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn). Thus, the lower science speaks of stones fixed in the fire / and of those which flee from the fire. Indeed, the stones which are called "stars" [fixed] are **gold**, **silver**, **lead**, **tin**, **iron**, **copper** <note>original: "es"; likely referring to copper or bronze</note> / and the true **carbuncle** <note>a deep red gemstone, often used as a metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone</note> and **emerald**, and the rest of the stones which do not flee from the fire. However, the stones which are called "planets" [wandering/volatile] are **quicksilver** <term>argentum vivum</term>, **sulfur**, **arsenic**, **sal ammoniac**, **tutia** <note>zinc oxide, often collected as a powder from furnace flues</note>, **magnesia**, and **marcasite**. For these do not persevere over the fire, but gradually and bit by bit they flee from it and depart to proceed into <unclear>vapor</unclear> in immutable bonds and by the steps of the arts in an indissoluble composition, which knowledge <term>perscientia</term> perceives with untiring natural remedy through divine omnipotence; that which operates through manifold deceits of labors / and through the doubts of works, the soul of knowledge perfects with foreknown perfection. He left this art to his son as a father to a son. For another, who is said to have been <unclear>Bin</unclear> <note>possibly a reference to "Ibn" (e.g., Ibn Sina or Ibn Hayyan), indicating the Arabic origins of much medieval alchemical lore</note>, accepted this especially, [whom] the skilled among the Indians consider to have begotten it.

It frames alchemical knowledge as a sacred, technical science of "meteorology" passed down through lineages, possibly citing Arabic or Indian origins.</summary>

### Concerning the fixation of the stone by night and by fire

<margin>Concerning the fixation of the stone by night through fire</margin>
Let them also look upon their aforementioned stones over the fire by night; and if they hold their colors from the fire throughout the night, namely, [it is well], and if not, then it will be a <unclear>sign</unclear> of the aforementioned power which <term>onotria</term> <note>original: "onotria"; likely a reference to Oenothera or "evening primrose," a plant whose name was etymologically linked to "wine-seeker" or "taming beasts," used here perhaps to describe a substance that "tames" the volatile spirits</note> lies at the feet of the quickened and vivifying spirits. In the work, you will thus find them. And this is accomplished over many years of their own labor by themselves, since they are all dug out from their own sources. And I know this as an answer to all my [labors] in a single day. You see how God favors [the work], namely in a single hour. For it is done with a medium with the body; many times they anticipate [the result] through it. They say that fixed stones, if <unclear>boiled</unclear> in fire, receive those things fleeing from it. Because [the fire] provides for them by itself; otherwise, in the end, they rule through the stones fixed over the fire without it persisting by itself. And by chance, they can kindle their virtues in the mouth <note>likely referring to the "opening" or "orifice" of a vessel</note>, for when it has been joined with spirits, just like stones fleeing from the fire, they cannot demonstrate their virtues—that is, until they have been changed with the fixed substances. Know, therefore, that this is a great secret.

### Concerning the change of bodies and spirits

<margin>Concerning the change of bodies and spirits</margin>
Know also that the aforementioned do not receive each other well unless they have first been changed in this work. For gold does not receive the spirit <note>in alchemy, "spirits" are volatile substances like mercury or sulfur, while "bodies" are solid metals</note> into itself unless it first becomes spiritual-corporeal as an essence—that is, purged of all filth and clarified in its body through the spirit, namely through the spirit itself, pure from those perfected works; and with the <unclear>filaments</unclear> of quicksilver <note>original: "argenti vivi"; mercury</note> in <unclear>exaltation</unclear> <note>a process by which a substance is raised to a higher state of purity or power</note> it is held by itself and binds [the spirit] from the bodies over the fire. Know, therefore, that this is a great secret.

<margin>You see that in one day I do more in this great art</margin>
Therefore, everyone who ignores God alone in His creation, let him stay his hand from the work, as the word itself says. For he who ignores Him does not rejoice in works without price. Provided that he knows, he will greatly rejoice in the works according to his desired wishes. I ask you, therefore, my son, that you run through the books with ineffable love, so that from those threads and from those [books] you may be able to wonder at knowing even more. For he who is lazy in reading books cannot be substantial in performing things. And no one will believe in a light hand in painting whose mind is not in the invention. Therefore, those to whom knowledge comes are not full of the enigmas of my work.

So you, my most dear son <note>original: "fili kme" for "fili carissime"; a common paternal address in alchemical texts</note>, leaving aside the philosophy already touched upon, since I have published this new book of mine from the [teachings] of the rabbi of [Pet?] <note>The Latin "ex rabu de pēt" is obscure; it likely refers to a specific Hebrew source or teacher the author is citing</note>. <margin>All things are held in every thing</margin>

Or, in the aforementioned bodies and spirits, certain destructible things themselves lose their own [nature] and are separated from the elementary instrumental sounds <note>referring to the harmonic proportions of the four elements</note>. I do not call them "elemented" things unless they retain their qualities: two active and two passive, both externally and internally, in softness or in hardness, as a medium holds them. But if externally a thing is hot and moist and soft, then internally it is cold, dry, and hard. Because the manifest part of every thing is the opposite of its secret part. Know this well.

See if you will easily recognize the qualities of the appearances of things of perfect generation... and if the hidden is made manifest, know that the manifest things are being hidden. But in some things, the whole manifest is hidden and the whole hidden is manifested, only so that they may reach perfect purity or at least be brought to the benefit of <term>neutrality</term> <note>original: "neutralitatem"; a balanced state where none of the four qualities—hot, cold, moist, or dry—dominates the others</note>. Know this: that it is a great secret in all things.

<margin>All in the health and destruction of things</margin>
Know also that of things, some are for **the health and destruction of things**, or that which is neutralized through this likeness of glory and glories, with softness in hardness, so constituted externally that the body is not separated from its own nature, as in perfect gold. In gold itself, indeed, it can be known that neutrality sublimes another. Behold, I have said for whom this preparation is meant.

There are few things through which the <term>proportion</term> <note>original: "proportio"; the mathematical ratio or balance of elements required for a successful reaction</note> of knowledge is impressed. One such way is through transmutation, so that things may be healed by alchemical and medical remedies—specifically, so that white gold and pure silver may be restored through the singular fixation of the components within their foundations. It is achieved through a true composition.
